## otakumode.com (Information; Tokyo Otaku Mode, Otapedia, English)

- https://otakumode.com/otapedia/games/final_fantasy/manga -- list of 8 manga -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - seven are entries (Aloha Iroha, Figaro's Wedding, Rikodoki, Lost Stranger, XII ~Manga~, Professor Lalafell, Legend of the Eternal Wind), recorded in their own sources files
  - the eighth is Bravely Default: Flying Fairy (see Decisions)
- https://otakumode.com/otapedia/games/final_fantasy/encyclopaedia_eorzea -- list of 4 books -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - Encyclopaedia Eorzea (the Final Fantasy XIV lore book, 8 books, over 300 pages, by Banri Oda, Michael-Christopher Koji Fox and Natsuko Ishikawa, supervised by Naoki Yoshida), The Sky: The Art of Final Fantasy (Amano and others, Dark Horse 2012), Dawn: The Worlds of Final Fantasy (Amano, Dark Horse 2009) and the Final Fantasy Ultimania guidebooks (about 72 companion books)
- https://otakumode.com/otapedia/games/final_fantasy/timeline -- dates -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - the main-series release timeline, 25 titles from Final Fantasy (1987) to the VII Remake (planned 2020-04-10, as of February 2020), plus the Square and Enix merger (2003-04-01) and the 20th Anniversary projects (2007 and 2008, with the 20th Anniversary Ultimania in 2008)
  - the page says Final Fantasy 1987-12-17 (en.wikipedia's Japanese release date is 1987-12-18) and Crystal Chronicles 2004-02-15 (the Japanese release is 2003-08-08); both are candidates in docs/wiki-corrections.md
- https://otakumode.com/otapedia/games/final_fantasy/switch_games -- Nintendo Switch games -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - its list of 8 Final Fantasy games on the Switch: Chocobo's Mystery Dungeon Every Buddy!, Final Fantasy Adventure (from Collection of Mana), Final Fantasy VII, VIII, IX, X/X-2, XV Pocket Edition and Crystal Chronicles
  - the page repeats the stale and wrong dates of its game pages (VIII "1997", IX "1999", X "2003", the VII Remake "3/3/2020", the Crystal Chronicles remake "planned" for 2020)
- https://steelseries.com/blog/final-fantasy-titles-switch-123 -- Nintendo Switch games -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - "All Final Fantasy Titles on Switch, Ranked", an opinion ranking; the source the Otapedia switch_games list was copied from (found by the user, 2026-10-17)
  - the full list is Final Fantasy XV: Pocket Edition, Chocobo's Mystery Dungeon Every Buddy!, Final Fantasy Adventure (from Collection of Mana), World of Final Fantasy: Maxima, Final Fantasy VII (1997), X/X-2, IX and XII: The Zodiac Age, with Final Fantasy VIII and Crystal Chronicles as honourable mentions
  - it has two games the Otapedia copy dropped: World of Final Fantasy Maxima and The Zodiac Age
- https://otakumode.com/otapedia/games/final_fantasy/android_games -- Android and iOS games -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - the page names iOS releases too, so it serves both platforms
  - its recommended picks (Dissidia Final Fantasy Opera Omnia, Brave Exvius, Final Fantasy Legends: Toki no Suisho, Dimensions I and II, Record Keeper) and its list of mobile releases from 2011 to 2020; every game on it is an entry here
  - "Toki no Suisho" is Dimensions II's Japanese title (listed twice on the page)
  - the page's "Final Fantasy XI on iOS and Android" (2019) has no matching release: en.wikipedia (Final Fantasy XI) says a mobile client was in development by Square Enix with the Korean developer Nexon on Unreal Engine 4 and was cancelled in late 2020, so it was never released (ours, 2026-10-17; recorded in xi-group.md); the XI group's Grandmasters is the 2015 mobile tie-in
  - its "Lightning Returns: Final Fantasy IX" is a slip for Lightning Returns: Final Fantasy XIII
  - its 2020 "Final Fantasy XV MMORPG" is a rumour (JSC Games and GAEA, Unreal Engine 4) that matches the XV group's War for Eos
  - the page was written before the 2020 releases
- https://otakumode.com/otapedia/games/final_fantasy/psp_games -- PSP games -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - its list of Final Fantasy PSP games: Dissidia Final Fantasy, Dissidia 012, Final Fantasy Tactics, Final Fantasy: War of the Lions, Crisis Core: Final Fantasy VII, Final Fantasy II; every game on it is an entry
- https://www.alteredgamer.com/final-fantasy/111540-ff-the-best-games-on-the-psp/ -- PSP games -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - "Best FF Games on PSP", an opinion list of four games (Crisis Core: Final Fantasy VII, Dissidia 012 Final Fantasy, Final Fantasy Tactics: The War of the Lions, Final Fantasy II); the source of the Otapedia PSP list, per the user
- https://otakumode.com/otapedia/games/final_fantasy/derivative_games -- derivative games -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - Otapedia's lists of derivative games, grouped as the mainline derivatives (X-2, VII Snowboarding, XII Revenant Wings, IV The After Years, XIV A Realm Reborn, VII G-Bike, Grandmasters), the Tactics series (Tactics, Advance, A2, S), the Compilation of Final Fantasy VII (Before Crisis, Dirge of Cerberus, Crisis Core), Fabula Nova Crystallis (Type-0, XIII-2, Lightning Returns, Agito, Awakening), the Final Fantasy XV Universe (Platinum Demo, Justice Monsters Five, A King's Tale, A New Empire, King's Knight, Monster of the Deep, Pocket Edition) and 28 spin-off games and series (Adventure, Mystic Quest, the Chocobo series, the Crystal Chronicles series, the Crystal Defenders series, the Dissidia series, Unlimited with U, The 4 Heroes of Light, Dimensions and Dimensions II, Airborne Brigade, Theatrhythm and Curtain Call, Artniks and Artniks Dive, All the Bravest, World Wide Words, Record Keeper, Explorers, Mobius, Brave Exvius, World of Final Fantasy); every title on it is an entry here
  - the Chocobo series is described as "more than 15 different releases" (1997 to present)
  - its Explorers text says up to 4 players co-op

- https://otakumode.com/otapedia/games/final_fantasy/ultimania -- Ultimania books -- accessed 2026-10-17
  - (page text pasted by user; Tokyo Otaku Mode's Otapedia, sections the series overview, Book Style, List of Works)
  - the Final Fantasy Ultimania series of guidebooks and artbooks by Studio BentStuff, supervised by Square Enix: the Final Fantasy 20th Anniversary Ultimania (Japan, January to June 2008, covering the first twelve games and X-2), the Final Fantasy 25th Memorial Ultimania (Japan, December 18, 2012, three volumes covering the first fourteen games, more of an artbook), and the English Final Fantasy Ultimania Archive (Dark Horse Comics, volume 1 on July 24, 2018, French and German editions since) with Volume 1 (I to VI), Volume 2 (VII to IX) and Volume 3 (X to XIV), each over 300 pages
  - a total of 72 companion books so far, mostly one per game
  - the books hold character profiles, art (Amano's sketches), story synopses, design notes and creator retrospectives, with unlocalized trivia such as the name of Emperor Mateus's mother, Aile
  - **no comics or novellas are mentioned**
  - its page text also mentions Dawn and The Sky (Dark Horse), see Decisions

## play.google.com (Platform; Google Play, developer page)
- https://play.google.com/store/apps/dev?id=8026010256970589886 -- the "Square Enix" developer's app list, visited and audited -- accessed 2026-10-20 (list pasted by user; every app on it counts as live)
  - 21 apps, ids under https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=: com.square_enix.android_googleplay.DD (Dissidia Duellum Final Fantasy); .ff7ecww (Final Fantasy VII Ever Crisis, now end of service); .FFPR, .FFPR2, .FFPR3, .FFPR4, .FFPR5, .FFPR6 (the Pixel Remaster games I to VI); .SagaCollection_Sww (Collection of SaGa Final Fantasy Legend); .FFVIII (VIII Remastered); .FFCCREww (Crystal Chronicles); .ffxivcomapp_n (XIV Companion); .ffl_gp (Dimensions); .ffl2w (Dimensions II); .FFVII (VII); .FFT_en2 (Tactics: The War of the Lions); .FF4AY_GP (IV: The After Years); .FFIV_GP (IV 3D Remake); .FFIII_GP (III 3D Remake); com.square_enix.FFIXww.android_googleplay (IX for Android); com.square_enix.ffportal_w.googleplay (Final Fantasy Portal App)
- https://web.archive.org/web/20210805055030/https://play.google.com/store/apps/collection/cluster?clp=igM4ChkKEzgwMjYwMTAyNTY5NzA1ODk4ODYQCBgDEhkKEzgwMjYwMTAyNTY5NzA1ODk4ODYQCBgDGAA%3D%3AS%3AANO1ljIRTeg&gsr=CjuKAzgKGQoTODAyNjAxMDI1Njk3MDU4OTg4NhAIGAMSGQoTODAyNjAxMDI1Njk3MDU4OTg4NhAIGAMYAA%3D%3D%3AS%3AANO1ljIKkik -- the same developer's "More by SQUARE ENIX Co.,Ltd." app list, Wayback capture of 2021-08-05, before the Pixel Remaster apps: visited and audited -- accessed 2026-10-20 (list pasted by user)
  - Final Fantasy apps on it: XIV Companion, FFBE War of the Visions, Brave Exvius, Dissidia Opera Omnia, Final Fantasy, II and III (old ports, no Pixel Remaster branding), VIII Remastered, Crystal Chronicles, XV Pocket Edition, Dimensions and Dimensions II, VII, IX for Android, Adventures of Mana, Portal App, Tactics: WotL, IV: The After Years, All the Bravest, IV (3D Remake), III (3D Remake)
  - Other Square Enix apps on it (out of scope): NieR Re[in]carnation, Dragon Quest and SaGa games, Mana, Chrono Trigger, The World Ends With You and others
  - No V or VI app on it, so those old Android ports were delisted before 2021-08
- https://web.archive.org/web/20150615154752/https://play.google.com/store/apps/dev?id=8026010256970589886 -- the same developer page, the very first capture of this URL in the old Play Store layout, 2015-06-15: audited -- accessed 2026-10-20 (list pasted by user, Chinese-language page)
  - Final Fantasy apps shown: Final Fantasy Tactics: WotL, Final Fantasy VI, Final Fantasy IV, 最終幻想 (the Chinese title of Final Fantasy, the first game), Final Fantasy All the Bravest; other Square Enix apps: Snack Truck Fever, Chaos Rings III, Dragon Quest, Chrono Trigger, Square Enix Software Token
  - A ten-app sample, not the full catalog (no II, III or V, and not the Portal App, whose first Play page capture is 2015-09-05 and whose worldwide date is 2015-08-19); the store used another UI and URL before this capture

- https://play.google.com/store/apps/dev?id=8026010256970589886&hl=ja&gl=JP -- the same developer page in the Japanese store, visited and audited -- accessed 2026-10-20 (list pasted by user; every app on it counts as live)
  - Final Fantasy apps on it: Brave Exvius, VII Ever Crisis, DDFF (Dissidia Duellum), XIV Companion, Final Fantasy VII, Final Fantasy (original, Pixel Remaster), II, III, IV, V and VI (Pixel Remaster titles), VIII Remastered, Crystal Chronicles, IX, Final Fantasy Legends and Legends II (the Japanese names of Dimensions and Dimensions II), Final Fantasy Gaiden Seiken Densetsu (Adventures of Mana), Final Fantasy IV TAY, IV (3D Remake), III (3D Remake), Tactics (Lions' War), Final Fantasy Portal App (a separate listing from the Square Enix App), Square Enix App, Sa-Ga Collection (the Legend collection)
  - Also listed, not Final Fantasy (out of scope): Dragon Quest, Octopath, Romancing SaGa, SaGa, Voice of Cards, Paranormasight, Mana, Chrono Trigger, Last Remnant, Valkyrie Profile, Chaos Rings III, Software Token and others
  - Not on it, as expected: G-Bike, Grandmasters, Tactics S, Agito, Explorers-Force, Meli-Melo, Pictlogica, World Wide Words, Artniks, Artniks Dive, Awakening, A New Empire, War for Eos, Justice Monsters Five, Mobius, Record Keeper, Airborne Brigade, Heavenstrike Rivals, Opera Omnia, Digital Card Game, Crystal Defenders (all delisted)
  - Ever Crisis ended in Japan too (end-of-service notice, 2026-10-07), and Brave Exvius's Japanese app is now an offline Memorial Version, which does not count as content (see Decisions)
  - The Final Fantasy Portal App is the same app in both stores (the Japanese listing is the global one, localized); its Triple Triad content is gone. The package com.square_enix.android_googleplay.PortalAppjp is a different app, the Square Enix App, with no Triple Triad text
  - The first screen of the live page shows only ten apps; the pasted list is the full set

- https://web.archive.org/web/20150926110212/https://play.google.com/store/apps/dev?id=8026010256970589886&hl=ja -- the developer page, Japanese layout, capture of 2015-09-26: audited -- accessed 2026-10-20 (list pasted by user)
  - Featured Heavenstrike Rivals; apps shown: Final Fantasy Tactics: WotL, Snack Truck Fever, Final Fantasy VI, Dragon Quest, Chrono Trigger, Dragon Quest IV, Final Fantasy, Final Fantasy IV, Final Fantasy V (so the Android port of V was on sale by 2015-09), Final Fantasy Portal App

## square-enix.co.jp/mobile/sem (Platform; Square Enix Mobile, Japan)

- https://web.archive.org/web/20070704192702/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/mobile/sem/ -- the site's title list, one capture per year, visited and audited -- accessed 2026-10-24 (pages saved by user; the capture of 2007-10-06 named in the audit request was not read, the first capture, 2007-07-04, was)
  - スクエニMobile: Square Enix's paid content service for phones in Japan, on i-mode (NTT DoCoMo), EZweb (au) and Yahoo!ケータイ (SoftBank); it ended all services on 2018-03-31 (https://www.jp.square-enix.com/announce/semobile/, the live notice, identical to the capture of 2018-04-02)
  - Captures read: 2007-07-04 (13 titles), 2008-07-06 (21), 2008-09-17, 2009-01-22, 2009-07-12 (35), 2009-08-30 (the ranking Flash page), 2010-01-14 (38), 2011-01-19 and 2012-01-19 (Flash index), 2014-07-06
  - The index page's HTML is unchanged from 2010-09-20 to 2017-11-18 (the Wayback index; the last capture before the shutdown notice is 2017-11-18); the 2015, 2016 and 2017 captures were not read
  - The Flash index reads its tiles from `swf/xml/images.xml`, which has one 200 capture, 2012-10-21; every Flash capture shows that list (21 tiles), so the tile list is the 2012-10-21 one
  - Scripts and data files on a saved page come from a later capture than the page: news and carrier boxes can be from 2011 or 2012
  - Titles with Chocobo, Moogle or Final Fantasy in the name or premise, with details in their own sources files:
    - ぱたぱたチョコボ (free Flash, 2007) and five ranking Flash games with Chocobo in the title (2008): [chocobo-game-series-1997.md](chocobo-game-series-1997.md)
    - チョコボdeモバイル (EZweb 2006-12-14, i-mode 2007-08-01): [chocobo-game-series-1997.md](chocobo-game-series-1997.md)
    - Four ranking Flash games with Moogle, Bomb and Cactuar (2008): [final-fantasy-minigames-2005.md](final-fantasy-minigames-2005.md)
    - 魔界塔士サ・ガ (i-mode 2007-07-02, EZweb 2007-12-13, SoftBank 2008-03-12): [the-final-fantasy-legend-i-iii-1989.md](the-final-fantasy-legend-i-iii-1989.md)
    - 聖剣伝説 -ファイナルファンタジー外伝- (distribution resumed 2011-05-18 and 2011-05-19): [final-fantasy-adventure-1991.md](final-fantasy-adventure-1991.md)
    - ファイナルファンタジー in いただきストリート MOBILE (2010-07-01), renamed 2011-02-03: [itadaki-street-party-games-2004.md](itadaki-street-party-games-2004.md)
  - MONOTONE: original RPG for keitai, concept design by Tetsuya Nomura (https://web.archive.org/web/20080519043806/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/mobile/mono/, Flash site: story, system and delivered-episodes pages) -- accessed 2026-10-24
    - Story: a boy names himself the last hero in a world losing "color" and "sound" to a Demon King
    - Command battle (attack, magic, item); art is not Final Fantasy's
    - The page names no Final Fantasy content
    - Moved to the スクエニMobile site 2007-11-01
  - TOBAL M (トバルM): 3D fighting action game, a revival of the トバル series for keitai (https://web.archive.org/web/20080528183543/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/mobile/sem/tobal/, Flash intro and the carrier boxes) -- accessed 2026-10-24
    - Carriers: i-mode 2007-12-12, EZweb 2008-08-07, from 500 points (525 yen)
    - The Flash intro (`index.swf`, not saved) shows only トバル characters; the page names no Final Fantasy content
    - Not Final Fantasy: left out
  - ポケットロジック and ポケットラビリンス: free Flash picture puzzles (copyright 2006 and 2007, puzzles by たきせ あきひこ), from the paid-app series スクエニ ポケットパズル (https://web.archive.org/web/20070109185249/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/mobile/game/pocket/pocket_puzzle.html, first capture listing ロジックパズル vol.1; https://web.archive.org/web/20080429140736/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/mobile/game/pocket/pocket_puzzle.html, the last before the end notice) and the Flash page (https://web.archive.org/web/20080615171012/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/mobile/sem/flash/index.html) -- accessed 2026-10-24
    - Every page says only that "familiar Square Enix characters appear" in the puzzles; none names Final Fantasy or any character
    - The screenshots (`pl_title.jpg`, `pl_game.jpg`, `plabylinth_title.jpg`, `plabylinth_game.jpg`) have no Wayback capture; the screenshots that load show no Final Fantasy content (user)
    - The paid-app version is listed as ロジックパズル vol.1 (20×20 grid, colour image revealed on completion) and ポケットラビリンス vol.1, on Yahoo!ケータイ and iモード
    - Not Final Fantasy: left out
  - Other titles listed on the site, not Final Fantasy: ポートピア連続殺人事件, 北海道連鎖殺人オホーツクに消ゆ, 軽井沢誘拐案内, 武蔵伝II EPISODE ZERO, 倉庫番, ルービックキューブ, ぱにぽに大富豪, ハガレン15パズル, ヘキサイト, どこでもソリティア, どこでも神経衰弱, 地獄のハイウェイ, ナインプレイス, スターオーシャン ブルースフィア, 国破れて山河あり (and 2), ロマンシング サガ (and 2), 元祖 半熟英雄, DEMON CHAIN, ロンゴロンゴ, グイン・サーガmobile, It's NEW FRONTIER, クロノ・トリガー, 聖剣伝説2, 伝説のオウガバトル, ワンダープロジェクト J2, シグマハーモニクス コーダ, いたストMOBILE, スクモバスケジューラー, アイテム

- http://dlgames.square-enix.com/na/ps3_dl/ -- "PlayStation3 DL delivery" menu, captures of 2010-11-29 (https://web.archive.org/web/20101129034233/http://dlgames.square-enix.com/na/ps3_dl/) and 2026-05-10 (the last, https://web.archive.org/web/20260510210031/http://dlgames.square-enix.com/na/ps3_dl/) -- visited and audited -- accessed 2026-10-24 (raw HTML read by me)
  - Lists CRYSTAL DEFENDERS (2009.10.29, the PSP date; the PS3 releases are 2009-03-11 in Japan and 2009-07-23 and 2009-08-06 in the West), the only Final Fantasy title on either capture
  - The official site http://www.crystaldefenders.jp/ (capture of 2010-11-29) holds only the title

## dlgames.square-enix.com (Platform; iPod / iPhone, North America)

- http://dlgames.square-enix.com/na/ipod/ -- "iPod / iPhone" menu, capture of 2026-04-22 (the last): https://web.archive.org/web/20260422231557/http://dlgames.square-enix.com/na/ipod/ -- visited and audited -- accessed 2026-10-24 (raw HTML read by me)
  - 40 apps, 2008.07.08 to 2013.03.28; Final Fantasy and Chocobo titles on it: Final Fantasy, II, III, IV, V, Tactics: The War of the Lions, Dimensions, Theatrhythm, Chocobo Panic, Voice Fantasy, Crystal Defenders and Plus, Vanguard Storm (each game's dates and detail pages are in its own sources file)
  - Gallery and wallpaper apps and an AR app (Advent Children Complete, Dissidia, Final Fantasy XIII, VII Compilation, Soul Eater and Fullmetal Alchemist galleries; Dissidia 012 AR): not games, left out
    - Dissidia 012 AR: a viewer for the PSP game's character models, not a game and not Dissidia 012 itself; evidence in sources/dff-group.md

    - https://web.archive.org/web/20210627002414/http://dlgames.square-enix.com/ff/ -- last working capture of the menu (2021-06-27); same list as the earlier captures; later captures redirect to the Japanese Pixel Remaster site -- accessed 2026-10-24

## square-enix.co.jp/smart/market (Platform; Square Enix Market, Japan)

- http://www.square-enix.co.jp/smart/market/ -- SQUARE ENIX MARKET, Square Enix's portal for Android phones in Japan, first working capture 2012-02-04: https://web.archive.org/web/20120204205708/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/smart/market/ -- visited and audited -- accessed 2026-10-25 (raw HTML read by me; a capture of 2016-03-13, https://web.archive.org/web/20160313074848/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/smart/market/, also loads and was not read)
  - Reached through the NTT docomo d menu and the au one menu; the service's address is http://sqmk.jp; au phones from 2011-12-01, docomo phones from 2012-01-23 (the page's news list)
  - Seven titles: Final Fantasy, Final Fantasy II, Crystal Defenders, Chrono Trigger, Dragon Quest Monsters WANTED!, Chaos Rings and Itadaki Street for SMARTPHONE
  - Final Fantasy: release 2011-12-01; detail page game003.html
  - Final Fantasy II: release 2012-02-01, with the two EX dungeons of earlier ports (Soul of Rebirth, Arcane Labyrinth); detail page game007.html
  - Crystal Defenders: release 2011-12-01, the Android version holds W1 and W2; detail page game004.html
  - Itadaki Street for SMARTPHONE: not examined for Final Fantasy content
  - Capture of 2013-02-16 (https://web.archive.org/web/20130216030731/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/smart/market/, given by user, not read) adds Final Fantasy Tactics: The Lion War (game016.html), Final Fantasy Legends (Dimensions) (game012.html) and Final Fantasy III (game008.html)
  - Capture of 2014-02-17 (https://web.archive.org/web/20140217005214/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/smart/market/, given by user, not read) adds Final Fantasy VI, Final Fantasy IV: The After Years (3D), Pictlogica Final Fantasy, Final Fantasy V, Final Fantasy IV and Final Fantasy All the Bravest; the title pages are on an.sqexm.net (/sp/site/Page/sqmk/ff6/top, ff4ay, pictlogica, ff5, ff4, ffatb)
  - Capture of 2016-03-02 (https://web.archive.org/web/20160302005440/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/smart/market/, given by user, not read) adds Final Fantasy Brave Exvius (an.sqexm.net/sp/site/Page/sqmk/ffbe/top)
  - Capture of 2017-06-06 (https://web.archive.org/web/20170606071609/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/smart/market/, given by user, not read) adds Final Fantasy IX (an.sqexm.net/sp/site/Page/sqmk/ff9/top)
  - Title list of 2023-02-04 (https://web.archive.org/web/20230204080504/http://an.sqexm.net/sp/site/Page/comm/sys/titlelist, given by user, not read) labels Final Fantasy III and Final Fantasy IV as 3D remakes (an.sqexm.net/sp/site/Page/sqmk/FF3/top, /ff4/top)
  - Last capture checked: the title list of 2026-07-12 (https://web.archive.org/web/20260712081254/http://an.sqexm.net/sp/site/Page/comm/sys/titlelist, given by user, not read)
  - Live title list, checked by the user 2026-10-25: https://an.sqexm.net/sp/site/Page/comm/sys/titlelist (not read by me); the Final Fantasy titles it lists as currently sold: Final Fantasy Brave Exvius, Final Fantasy IX, Final Fantasy IV (3D Remake), Final Fantasy IV: The After Years (3D), Final Fantasy III (3D Remake), Final Fantasy Legends (Dimensions) and Final Fantasy Tactics: The Lion War
  - Audit cadence: yearly captures are a first-pass spot check; titles can come and go between captures
  - A title's first capture only shows it was listed by then, not when it was added or removed; the exact date is the 発売日 field on a detail page, where one was read
  - Detail pages read from the capture of 2012-02-04 (https://web.archive.org/web/20120204205708/http://www.square-enix.co.jp/smart/market/game003.html, game007.html, game004.html)

## Decisions

Also recorded in the group files named in each item:

- **Not to be listed: collector and special-edition bundles (user, 2026-10-24):** boxed sets, anniversary boxes and collector editions of games that are sold separately; not widely sold, out of print, and dearer than the games bought separately, so they do not help a reader find the games
  - A standard omnibus that sold widely and whose name does not say what is in it is listed as an edition (Final Fantasy I & II: Dawn of Souls)
  - Applied to: the XIII/XIII-2 Dual Pack, the 25th Anniversary Ultimate Box and the XIII Ultimate Collection (see sources/xiii-group.md)
- **Not to be listed: Final Fantasy XIV: Libra Eorzea (user, 2026-10-20):** a free smartphone app by Square Enix (ファイナルファンタジーXIV: ライブラ エオルゼア), iOS 2013-09-10 and Android 2013-11-01 in all regions, giving offline access to the official XIV database and a character profile viewer for gear and attributes; discontinued 2019-04-23 and supplanted by the Final Fantasy XIV Companion
  - A different app, not a version of the Companion: its own store listing (iOS id 682415504, the Companion is id 1293636320) and a different function (an offline reference database and profile viewer, not the Companion's chat, market board and subscription service)
  - It does not meet the content bar: the database is encyclopedia-like content and the profile viewer is too minor a tool for XIV players; and as a discontinued app there is nothing left to load, and no experience to gain from an old build or old videos of it
  - The Companion stays (it barely meets the bar, but it is an important tool and the subscription service for XIV players)
  - Evidence: https://breezewiki.com/finalfantasy/wiki/Final_Fantasy_XIV:_Libra_Eorzea (the Final Fantasy Wiki page, pasted by user 2026-10-20) and the official end-of-service notice, http://na.finalfantasyxiv.com/pr/special/libraeorzea/ (still live; "As of April 23, 2019, service for FINAL FANTASY XIV: Libra Eorzea will be discontinued", pointing to the Companion and the Lodestone database)
  - Found on the Final Fantasy Wiki's iOS and iPadOS page (see the apps.apple.com section above) and in a 2014 Wayback capture of the Square Enix developer page on the App Store
- **Not to be listed: Knights of the Crystals (user, 2026-10-23):** a Square Enix social RPG for GREE phones in Japan (ナイツ オブ クリスタル, Japan 2010-08-10), on Facebook worldwide from 2010-11-02 and on iOS and Android from 2011-09-01, closed 2012-02-29; branded separately, not as a Final Fantasy title
  - It borrows the series' look: jobs (Warrior, Monk, White and Black Mage, Dark Knight, Dragoon), classic summons (Ifrit, Shiva, Bahamut), monsters such as the Lamia and the Imp, Final Fantasy lettering and a crystal in its logo, and a Final Fantasy Tactics-themed job card set (August 2011); illustrated by Ryoma Ito (Final Fantasy Tactics Advance)
  - No official tie: the Final Fantasy Wiki page says it "was not formally released as a Final Fantasy title" and files it under Category: Related games (46 pages, mixing Dragon Quest, Persona 5 Royal, Fall Guys and other publishers' games); MobyGames, the game's own wiki and Kotaku do not mention a Final Fantasy link; Wikipedia's List of Final Fantasy video games, "Other games", does not list it
  - Its closest comparisons are Final Fantasy Adventure and The Final Fantasy Legend I–III, which stay listed because they were marketed as Final Fantasy games ("Final Fantasy" is in the title, so the list's hand is forced; the Legend games are one omnibus entry); Knights of the Crystals never was. Other Square Enix titles that are not Final Fantasy are already out of scope (see the Dragon Quest, SaGa and Mana line above)
  - Evidence: https://breezewiki.com/finalfantasy/wiki/Knights_of_the_Crystals (pasted by user 2026-10-23), https://www.4gamer.net/games/123/G012325/20101102028/ (the Facebook launch of 2010-11-02, Square Enix's own wording; official sites http://www.square-enix.co.jp/facebook/koc/ and apps.facebook.com/knightsofthecrystals/), https://kotaku.com/knights-of-the-crystals-is-final-fantasy-for-facebook-5679450, https://tvtropes.org/pmwiki/pmwiki.php/VideoGame/KnightsOfTheCrystals, https://www.mobygames.com/game/52030/knights-of-the-crystals/
- **Not to be listed: the Final Fantasy Brave Exvius Memorial Version (user, 2026-10-20):** the Japanese offline app Square Enix released after the game's shutdown, not a row on the Brave Exvius entry and not an entry of its own
  - The Japanese Android page com.square_enix.ffbejpn (updated Nov 19, 2025, "Memorial Version bug fixes", a roughly 6 GB resource re-download; page text pasted by user) and the Japanese iOS page id940099765 (live per the iTunes lookup, updated 2025-11-27)
  - Per an AI summary pasted by the user (not verified): the Japanese game ended on 2025-10-31 (the global game on 2024-10-31), and the Memorial Version is a post-service offline archive and viewer with no gameplay: the main story, character encyclopedias, unit galleries and enemy data, without full battle mechanics or collaboration content; there is no official English version
  - Why: it is not content, but a reference archive, like the XIV Libra Eorzea app (also not listed); the same summary names a fan-made English Memorial Edition by NightWaIker (Luminest), also not listed
- **Not to be listed: the cancelled 64DD version of Final Fantasy VII (user, 2026-11-03):** announced for the Nintendo 64 disk drive add-on and never released; Square moved the game to PlayStation (released 1997-01-31), so the 64DD version is not a version or platform of Final Fantasy VII on the list
  - Evidence: https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/NINTENDO_64のゲームタイトル一覧 (the 発売されなかったタイトル section: ファイナルファンタジーVII, スクウェア, 64DD, "PlayStationに移行。") and https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/64DD (the 移行して発売されたソフト section: released on PlayStation on 1997-01-31); details in sources/final-fantasy-vii-1997.md
- **Not to be listed: Fortress (user, 2026-11-03):** the whole game is left out, not a platform. The codename of a planned Xbox 360 spin-off, a sequel to the events of Final Fantasy XII, by Grin for Square Enix; production began in 2009, Square Enix withheld milestone payments over the art style, and Grin shut down six months in; never released
  - Evidence: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cancelled_Xbox_360_games (revision 1379151378 of 2026-10-08; Fortress row, citing GameSpot's 2015-10-06 article, https://web.archive.org/web/20151006174313/http://www.gamespot.com/articles/the-canceled-final-fantasy-xii-sequel-narrative-an/1100-6431040/); details in sources/xii-group.md
- **Not to be listed: Chocobo Racing 3D (user, 2026-11-03):** the whole game is left out, not a platform. A Nintendo 3DS sequel to Chocobo Racing announced at E3 2010, cancelled in 2013 (Square Enix, via Siliconera 2013-10-10) and explained in 2014 as a quality problem (Shinji Hashimoto, via Nintendo Life 2014-06-04); never released, and Chocobo GP (Nintendo Switch, 2022) is a separate game
  - Evidence: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cancelled_Nintendo_3DS_games (revision 1374058306 of 2026-09-09; Chocobo Racing 3D row); details in sources/chocobo-game-series-1997.md
- **Not to be listed: the cancelled WonderSwan Color version of Chocobo no Fushigi na Dungeon 2 (user, 2026-11-03):** a Square WonderSwan Color game in the cancelled list, cited to GameSpot's 'Square Wonderswan games update' (December 1999, http://www.gamespot.com/news/2447101.html); never released, so the WonderSwan Color is not a platform of Chocobo's Dungeon 2. Only this platform is left out: the game itself released on other platforms and stays listed on them
  - Evidence: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_WonderSwan_Color_games (revision 1351986427 of 2026-05-01; Cancelled games section); details in sources/chocobo-game-series-1997.md
- **Not to be listed: the cancelled WonderSwan Color version of Dice de Chocobo (user, 2026-11-03):** a Square WonderSwan Color remake of the PlayStation game, in the cancelled list; never released, so the WonderSwan Color is not a platform of Dice de Chocobo. Only this platform is left out: the game itself released on other platforms and stays listed on them
  - Evidence: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_WonderSwan_Color_games (revision 1351986427 of 2026-05-01; Cancelled games section); details in sources/chocobo-game-series-1997.md
- **Not to be listed: the cancelled WonderSwan Color version of Final Fantasy III (user, 2026-11-03):** a Square WonderSwan Color game in the cancelled list, cited to RPGFan's 'WSC FFIII Vanishes, FFI-II Remake In The Works' (2002, http://www.rpgfan.com/news/2002/1717.html, https://web.archive.org/web/20070315205600/http://www.rpgfan.com/news/2002/1717.html); never released, so the WonderSwan Color is not a platform of Final Fantasy III. Only this platform is left out: the game itself released on other platforms and stays listed on them
  - Evidence: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_WonderSwan_Color_games (revision 1351986427 of 2026-05-01; Cancelled games section); details in sources/final-fantasy-iii-1990.md
- **Not to be listed: the cancelled Famicom/NES version of Final Fantasy IV (user, 2026-11-03):** planned for the Famicom (Square, 1991, Japan) and moved to the Super Famicom before any work was done, per GamesRadar+ 'The lost Nintendo games' (2008-05-30, https://www.gamesradar.com/the-lost-nintendo-games/) and Lost Levels (2003-11, http://www.lostlevels.org/200311/200311-square.shtml); never released, so the NES is not a platform of Final Fantasy IV. Only this platform is left out: the game itself released on other platforms and stays listed on them
  - Evidence: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cancelled_NES_games (revision 1382879856 of 2026-10-30; Final Fantasy IV row); https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Nintendo_Entertainment_System_games (revision 1381703740 of 2026-10-24; Unreleased games section); details in sources/final-fantasy-iv-1991.md
- **Not to be listed: the cancelled SNES and Nintendo 64 versions of Final Fantasy VII (user, 2026-11-03):** work began on the SNES in 1994 and stopped when the staff finished Chrono Trigger (1995); early plans and technical tests followed for the Nintendo 64 and its 64DD add-on, then the game moved to PlayStation again (released 1997); no substantial work was finished, per Polygon's oral history (2017-01-09, https://www.polygon.com/a/final-fantasy-7); the SNES and Nintendo 64 are not platforms of Final Fantasy VII, as with the 64DD. Only these platforms are left out: the game itself released on PlayStation and stays listed on its released platforms
  - Evidence: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cancelled_Nintendo_64_games (revision 1383021177 of 2026-10-31; Final Fantasy VII row); https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cancelled_Super_NES_games (revision 1383237431 of 2026-11-01; Final Fantasy VII row); details in sources/final-fantasy-vii-1997.md
- **Not to be listed: the cancelled Nintendo Switch version of Final Fantasy XV (user, 2026-11-03):** director Hajime Tabata said Square Enix tried to run the game on the Switch in 2017 and could not get it performing well enough (IGN, 2017-09-04, https://www.ign.com/articles/2017/09/04/square-enix-tried-running-final-fantasy-xvs-engine-on-switch-it-didnt-work-well-enough); never released; the Switch has Final Fantasy XV: Pocket Edition (2018) instead, which is its own release, not this port. Only the Switch version of the main game is left out: Final Fantasy XV itself stays listed on its released platforms
  - Evidence: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cancelled_Nintendo_Switch_games (revision 1378713677 of 2026-10-05; Final Fantasy XV row); details in sources/final-fantasy-xv-2016.md
- **Not to be listed: Mario Hoops 3-on-3 and the Super Smash Bros. games (user, 2026-11-08):** Mario and Nintendo games with Final Fantasy characters as guests; they are not Final Fantasy games and are out of scope
  - Mario Hoops 3-on-3 (Nintendo DS, 2006; developed by Square Enix): five unlockable Final Fantasy characters (Black Mage, White Mage, Moogle, Ninja, Cactuar)
  - Super Smash Bros. for Nintendo 3DS and for Wii U (2014): Cloud as a downloadable fighter and a Midgar stage; Ultimate (Nintendo Switch, 2018) carries both on
  - Evidence: https://finalfantasywiki.com/wiki/Mario_Hoops_3-on-3, https://finalfantasywiki.com/wiki/Super_Smash_Bros._for_Nintendo_3DS, https://finalfantasywiki.com/wiki/Super_Smash_Bros._for_Wii_U, https://finalfantasywiki.com/wiki/Super_Smash_Bros._Ultimate (Category:Crossover games; accessed 2026-11-08); details in sources/other-group.md

Franchise file only:

- **Square Enix Market is not a listed platform (user, 2026-10-25):** a Japan-only Android store; it is audited for Final Fantasy and Chocobo titles the list lacks, and is reconsidered if it is the only way to obtain a title
- **Not to be listed: three more items from the Final Fantasy Wiki's iOS and iPadOS page (user, 2026-10-20):**
  - Distant Worlds: Music from Final Fantasy: an iOS app listed under the wiki's Applications, tied to the Distant Worlds concert series; excluded because it is music only
  - Square Enix Software Token: a security token (authenticator) app for Square Enix accounts; excluded: it is a useful tool for XIV players, but XIV's login page already steers players to two-factor authentication, so players are told about it, and people who don't play XIV have no need to discover it
  - Dive In (service): Square Enix's subscription cloud-streaming service in Japan, launched 2014-10-09 and shut down 2015-09-13, run on G-Cluster's framework to stream games to iOS, iPadOS and Android devices, each with a free 30-minute trial and timed access passes; excluded as a distribution platform (a Japan-only, short-lived one), the games stay listed on their own rows
    - Final Fantasy games on it, per the Final Fantasy Wiki: VII International, VIII, XIII and XIII-2 (dates and rows are in their sources files)
    - https://breezewiki.com/finalfantasy/wiki/G-Cluster (pasted by user 2026-10-20): G-Cluster was a Japan-only cloud gaming service (a G-Cluster device for HDTVs plus game-specific streaming apps on iOS, iPadOS and Android); Square Enix used parts of its technology for Dive In; founded in Finland in 2000, the global company went bankrupt and ended its service on 2016-05-23, and Japan's Broadmedia kept the platform until it was retired on 2025-02-28
    - Cloud services in Japan that carried FF games (user, 2026-10-22). This file keeps only what applies to a whole service; each title's dates, links and details are in its own sources file: final-fantasy-vii-1997.md, final-fantasy-viii-1999.md, final-fantasy-ix-2000.md, final-fantasy-x-2001.md, final-fantasy-x-2-2003.md, final-fantasy-xiii-2009.md, final-fantasy-xiii-2-2011.md, lightning-returns-final-fantasy-xiii-2013.md and final-fantasy-xv-2016.md
    - G-cluster (Broadmedia): a Japan-only cloud game service. The technology belonged to G-cluster Global Inc. (the 2013 to 2016 releases); later releases are by Broadmedia GC Inc., a Broadmedia subsidiary. The console went on sale on 2013-06-20 (https://ad.impress.co.jp/special/g-cluster1306/, an advertorial: a 45 g HDMI box played with a USB pad or a phone as the controller, with the T's TV video-rental service on the same box). Broadmedia defines the technology as server-side processing streamed as video, with the terminal only decoding (https://www.broadmedia.co.jp/news/2016/04/28-001404.html). New sales ended on 2024-08-30 at noon and the whole service on 2025-02-28 at noon (https://gcluster.jp/news/20240808.html), so every G-cluster row is struck
      - Where it ran: the console; TVs with it built in (Sharp AQUOS, LG Smart TV); a G-cluster app for Sony BRAVIA Android TV; Apple TV; the PIXELA Smart Box (Android TV OS set-top box, from December 2017, ended by 2022-12-05); COCORO GAME (Sharp's own service, ended 2021-05-31); phone and tablet apps for Android and iOS; the Amazon Android app store for Fire devices; and KDDI's au Game for Android until 2023-05-15, after which buyers moved to the Google Play versions. The free "G-cluster Remote" phone app turns a phone into the controller, which is what "Smart G-cluster" on the game pages meant. The vendors' guide and partnership pages name no FF title; which titles ran on which device is recorded in each title's sources file only where a source says so
      - Service-wide sources: the TV linkage page (https://web.archive.org/web/20170923211747/http://gcluster.jp/pc/app/sqex/connectTV.html, the devices it lists); the phone app news page (https://web.archive.org/web/20250114083821/https://gcluster.jp/sp/app/sqex/news.html, saved in .plan/gcluster.md: launch notices, the au Game notice and the end schedule); Broadmedia's news lists, https://www.broadmedia.co.jp/news/2016/, /2017/ and /2020/, which hold no other FF item in any year; the Broadmedia GC developer page on the App Store (https://web.archive.org/web/20191231113138/https://apps.apple.com/jp/developer/broadmedia-gc-corporation/id1144024684, the apps live on 2019-12-31)
      - FF titles on it: VII International, VIII, IX, X/X-2 HD Remaster, XIII, XIII-2 and Lightning Returns (the Final Fantasy Wiki's G-Cluster page lists the same seven; its icons exist only for the five that later had apps). Non-FF Square Enix titles: The Last Remnant and Murdered. Lookup note: an index of `/pc/app/sqex/*` on Wayback misses the older `/pc/games/` pages where VII and VIII live
    - Hikari TV Games (ひかりTVゲーム; NTT Plala, run by Docomo from 2022): cloud games for Hikari TV set-top boxes, with the same Square Enix builds as G-cluster, each title a few weeks to a few months after G-cluster's. FF titles: VII International, VIII, IX, XIII, XIII-2 and Lightning Returns, never X/X-2, from the service's FF feature page over its life (captures from 2014-11 to 2021-11: https://web.archive.org/web/20141111011733/http://game.hikaritv.net/feature/finalfantasy/index.html and https://web.archive.org/web/20211105102828/http://game.hikaritv.net/feature/finalfantasy/index.html). Only VII and VIII carry "for ひかりTV" in their titles; they were ports of the Windows releases and the first two released, which G-cluster mirrors with "for Gクラスタ". It also had an Android app (jp.co.plala.game, 2013-11-20 to 2016-08-31, https://web.archive.org/web/20140712194340/https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=jp.co.plala.game; the explainer's 2016-07-28, 2016-08-29 and 2016-09-29 captures show the end) that played only some titles and doubled as a controller; there was never an iOS app, the site never marked which titles ran on the app, and every title carries a TV badge, so phone use of the FF titles is unknown. The service ended 2023-09-30 at 23:59, with monthly-plan signups closed 2023-06-30 (https://www.hikaritv.net/info/2023/0301/index.html and https://www.docomo.ne.jp/info/news_release/2023/03/01_03.html); service site http://game.hikaritv.net/index.html; explainer https://web.archive.org/web/20130611031103/http://game.hikaritv.net/about/index.html; standalone phone games on it (にゃんにゃん, ルナたん) are separate native apps, not this
    - Pages: https://breezewiki.com/finalfantasy/wiki/Dive_In_(service) (pasted by user 2026-10-20) and the official site, https://web.archive.org/web/20150317010201/http://divein.jp.square-enix.com/
    - G-Cluster subscribers kept its titles until 2025-02-28, per the iOS page
    - https://gcluster.jp/news/20240808.html -- the G-cluster service-termination notice (found by the user, 2026-10-21): the whole cloud gaming service stops at noon on Friday 2025-02-28, so every G-cluster row is struck -- accessed 2026-10-21; archived: https://web.archive.org/web/20240809012957/https://gcluster.jp/news/20240808.html
    - Dive In has its own rows, `Dive In cloud`, struck, Japan only, linking the official site's capture (https://web.archive.org/web/20150317010201/http://divein.jp.square-enix.com/, which describes the DIVE IN Viewer app and the rental-style play passes but names no games); each sits on the same version as that game's `G-cluster cloud` row, the Windows port, since Dive In ran on G-cluster's framework with the same titles and no new ones (our matching: neither the wiki nor the Dive In page names builds; user, 2026-10-22). G-cluster's own phone apps have separate rows, `G-cluster cloud`
- **Japanese rows for terminated apps (user, 2026-10-20):** "terminated" can mean the content inside an app shut down while the app is still online; a struck global row stays on its own, and a live Japanese row is added only when the content is still live in the Japanese app (so the Portal App, Ever Crisis and Brave Exvius get none); the docs' regional-rows rule already says this and is unchanged
- **Not to be listed: Inside FINAL FANTASY (user, 2026-11-10):** a behind-the-scenes TV series (2019 to 2020, 7 episodes) of interviews with the series' creators, artists and developers; behind-the-scenes and developer content is out of scope (docs/data.md, "What belongs on a list"); details in sources/other-group.md
- **Not to be listed: FINAL FANTASY, BLACK MAGES LEGACY (user, 2026-11-10):** an announced anime about Vivi's children, likely cancelled and out of scope; details in sources/ix-group.md
- **Not to be listed: Let's Mosey: A Slow Translation Of Final Fantasy VII (user, 2026-11-10):** a playthrough with commentary (Tim Rogers, 2017 to 2019), out of scope, noted as a playthrough of note; details in sources/vii-group.md and sources/final-fantasy-vii-1997.md
- **Not to be listed: Beyond Final Fantasy (user, 2026-11-10):** a bonus DVD of the International and PAL Final Fantasy X (developer and voice-actor interviews, trailers, an art gallery, a music video); behind-the-scenes content is out of scope; details in sources/x-group.md
- **Not to be listed: The History of Final Fantasy (user, 2026-11-10):** a 2006 documentary on the series, a bonus of the Final Fantasy XII Collector's Edition; out of scope; details in sources/xii-group.md
